# The hourly autopilot round: standing rules, state, open work

The cloud session's hourly round (a scheduled task, minute 57) reads this file first and does what it says. Until
11 Nov 2026 the whole text lived in the scheduled task itself and a copy in the session's scratch folder; it is here
now so that the state is versioned with the code it describes and survives a new session. **Whoever changes the state
(a deploy, a result, a decision of the owner's) edits this file in the same commit.** No secret is in it: the ops key
is named by its path only.

Everything below the line is the round's instruction, kept as it was written for the round.

---

Autopilot round for snipetech: watch the tape, keep the record, keep the server's own agents (which run on the owner's Anthropic API credits) researching, refresh the owner's dashboard, and carry the work below to done. Server: SNIPETECH_OPS_URL=https://snipetech.45-77-66-186.sslip.io, SNIPETECH_OPS_KEY=/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-snipetech/e4403109-fa5e-5b10-9649-38580af733e4/scratchpad/ops_key (a PATH to the key file, not its contents); repo /home/user/snipetech (venv .venv; branch claude/stoic-babbage-5e6pck, kept at the pushed tip). CLAUDE.md holds the rules; docs/roadmap.md's top section the honest state; docs/live-recipe.md the record of every live trade; docs/audit-2026-11-11.md what the whole-system audit found, fixed, measured and left open (rows 1-76, "Still open" 1-21); docs/research/ the research record (README.md lists every report, newest last: the newest are of 12 Nov). Never touch /home/user/velo. Never paste keys. Commit trailers: the ones the session's attribution reminder gives, and Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01RfSmxbWoHM4X7omFdj338v. Never change RISK/AUTOTEST, never start, stop or arm a sniper by hand, never stop a sniper that holds a coin.

OWNER, 10 Nov 21:00: "Figure it all out urself." Do NOT hand the owner homework. Decide from the data and our own docs. The ONLY thing that goes to the owner is one clear yes/no before anything spends their SOL in a new way (a new live strategy, a size above 0.2 SOL, a change in how live buys are placed), with the numbers behind it. The owner offered 100 SOL "whenever, we just need some proof": proof needs 0.2-1 SOL trades; do not ask for it. The owner may say "stop them" to end the copy snipers that no longer qualify (7aPo3n, AG89fn); without those words leave them. Bring up a tool unprompted the moment it would unblock something.
OWNER'S MONEY MOVES (9 Nov 14:05): "Don't flag, I constantly take out and put money on there." Never report, flag or ask about SOL going in or out of the sniper wallet, an empty wallet, a balance change no trade of ours explains, or failed transactions from the owner's own transfers. Never ask for SOL.
OWNER, 11 Nov (six times): "Go through every single component ... make it 10/10 ... Find every small thing that is ruining speed or a bug or an error or something that should be happening that isnt. Perfect it." Keep doing exactly that; never claim it is perfect; say plainly what is open. The owner wants plain words and honest numbers, every claim checked against the data before it is said, and a wrong statement corrected in the same words. What paid off most on 11 Nov and is worth repeating: read the server agents' own turns (operator/turns.jsonl: every tool call and its result) and their idea list (operator/ideas.json) for what they waste, what they cannot do and what they wait on this session for; check every "this pays" analysis for its landing and its cost; ask of each regular job who reads its output.

WHO DOES WHAT (owner, 12 Nov, about 03:00 UTC): "Make sure you are fixing the system but the work is done through the api credits like the actual system/tool. So ur here to improve it but it does all the trading stuff and analysing etc". This session fixes and improves the system: its code, its bugs, its speed, its tools, the agents' instructions and the record. The system does the work, on the owner's API credits: the trading (OKTech, by its own rules and the owner's yes), the research, the labs, the studies and the reading of them. So a study, a lab run for its answer, a wallet hunt or a "does this pay" question goes to the server's agents as an idea (`operate idea`), with the tool built here when they lack one. This session starts a job on the server only to test or measure a change of its own (a deploy check, a profile, the before and after of a fix), to run a repair it built (a re-score), or to read state. Where an item of OPEN WORK says to run a study or a lab for its answer, read it as: make sure the agents can run it and do, and read what they found.

WHERE AGENT WORK RUNS (owner, 8 Nov 20:40, CHANGED 12 Nov: "Yes to everything" lifted the rule against helpers; CLAUDE.md holds it; the hourly trigger's stored text still says the old rule). Helpers may be used: the Agent tool in the background works while the owner is away, the Workflow and Artifact tools wait on an approval nobody gives. Research goes to the server's agents: `remote --wait run operate idea --title .. --why .. --test .. --expected .. --kind research --priority N` (title under 120 characters, why 700, test 650, expected 400 since the deploy of 15 Nov 00:20; `--id I51 --test ..` changes one). Code changes stay in this session, inline, tests first. `remote sql` stops at 30 s and 500 rows (never LIKE on trades). `remote --wait run` prints only the tail: for a whole job log use `.venv/bin/python /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/e4403109-fa5e-5b10-9649-38580af733e4/scratchpad/fetch_log.py $SNIPETECH_OPS_URL $SNIPETECH_OPS_KEY jobs/<id>.log <out file>`. Since the audit of 22 Nov `remote read jobs/<id>.log --tail N` does it without the script (N bytes from the file's end; with no `--tail`, its last 200 kB from a whole line), and the client says on stderr when a read, or the log a `--wait` or a `remote job` prints (its last 20 kB), is only the file's end: a cut log is never taken for the whole of it. Foreground `sleep N; cmd` is refused: wait with an until-loop. NEVER wrap `remote --wait run <long job>` in a retry loop: start a long job once with `remote run`, then poll `remote jobs`. Never run `ruff format` on `src tests` as a whole: only on the files changed, and compare `ruff check` counts per file with HEAD. Never grep the generated files under src/snipetech/solana/ys. Never edit a file under src/ or tests/ while the full suite runs (the parallel-lab tests compare the code on disk with the code imported and fail). PROFILE BEFORE OPTIMISING, on the server: `remote run --profile <command>` runs the command as it is and leaves where its time went in jobs/profile-<command>-<time>.log (read it with the fetch script or the ops `read`). In a profile a function's time includes its waits: discovery's sources share one database connection, and 293 s of waiting were read as the cost of launch lookups (11 Nov); it now runs its sources in turn when profiled. Check a fix against the next scheduled run's own time (`remote logs <unit>`: "Consumed ... CPU time") before writing that it helped. `operate --dry-run` keeps nothing since 11 Nov (it used to save its snapshot and swallow the next round's events). A job started through `remote run` or `remote update` is marked `by: remote` and wakes no agent. The full test suite takes 3 to 5 minutes: `(timeout 1500 .venv/bin/pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider > <scratchpad>/pytest_full.out 2>&1; echo "pytest_exit=$?" >> <scratchpad>/pytest_full.out) &` then `until grep -q pytest_exit <file>; do sleep 5; done`.

SERVER REACHABLE FIRST, every round: `.venv/bin/snipetech remote status`. Not reachable: wait 10 minutes and try again before saying anything (11 Nov 07:12-07:23 UTC the machine stood still for 11 minutes while Ubuntu's own update job held 15.6 GB; it came back alone and the tape replayed what it missed). Still unreachable after 30 minutes: tell the owner in two lines to restart it in Vultr, nothing else, at most every 3 hours. The status now says machine.memory (available, swap), machine.pressure, machine.stalled_secs (seconds waited since boot: two readings bracket a stall) and units_mem_gb (now and peak): after any stall read them and machine.other_units first.

DEPLOYS: only when `remote jobs` shows no lab, qualify, research search, rescore or paper freeze running (a lab running across a deploy falls back to one process). The database copy (`snapshot-db`, every three hours, 41 to 69 minutes on 18-20 Nov) holds no deploy once the server runs the code of 20 Nov 04:2x (it loads no code after it starts; a copy finished on old code lacks only what a deploy added, and readers read the live file for that until the next copy); before that, the server itself refuses the deploy while a copy runs: never `--force` past it. `remote update` restarts ops and the tape; the tape replays what it missed (no hole); jobs survive on their old code. Files go to the server with `remote put <file> <name>` (under inputs/). `remote run config ...` is refused. SINCE THE AUDIT OF 22 NOV an update whose install (pip) fails puts the checkout back on the commit it was on and restarts nothing: its job log ends "the install failed: the checkout is back on <commit> and nothing was restarted", exit 1, with no "deployed" line. So read the update's job log for its "deployed <commit>" line before writing that a deploy went out. `remote status` names `endpoint_code` (the commit the endpoint itself runs) beside `code` (the checkout's): the two differ until the restart.

LIVE RUNS THAT END (since the audit of 22 Nov; docs/live-recipe.md, "The ends of a live run", has the rules): a run started with `remote run oktech cycle` or `remote run oktech guard` is a job and dies with a restart of the machine. When such a run is over, however it ended, the server's round stops its snipers once the wallet holds nothing of ours (two readings, and no buy on the tape they did not show; since the audit's third round, 23 Nov, none on the chain either, and a transaction the node cannot serve yet holds the stops a round; since 23 Nov 14:3x the owner's own coins beyond doubt are no holding, as for /stop, strays still are, and beside an owner's coin a sniper with no fee record keeps running); while a coin of ours is held it names them to the owner with /sell, and the book and the live tests add nothing beside them until then (operator/leftovers.py, operator/leftovers.json; the round's log says `leftovers: ...`). So after a restart or a kill during a live run, read operator/leftovers.json and `oktech status` before starting anything, and leave the stopping to the round: it stops them once the wallet holds nothing (a stopped sniper strands its coin, CLAUDE.md rule 1). A new run started on snipers OKTech reads armed already (a dead run left them so) reads the wallet twice and looks for a buy on its way before its start stops them, and may refuse ("Start the run again in a minute"): start it again, never with --ignore-mint for that coin. `remote run oktech guard` gets its `--max-minutes` plus 25 minutes as its job limit (the 15-minute watch at its end and 10 over) and refuses to start under a limit shorter than its minutes plus that watch; `remote run oktech cycle` sets its limit from its flags as before.

A DEPLOY NO LONGER KILLS A JOB THAT STARTS DURING IT, AND A JOB HELD BACK FOR A DEPLOY IS NO FAILURE (server audit finding 8; tests/test_ops.py::test_no_job_starts_while_a_code_update_goes_out, tests/operator/test_standin.py::test_a_stage_held_back_by_a_waiting_deploy_is_no_failing_stage). At 03:30:50 the round's timer fired four seconds into the 03:30:45 deploy (the timer starts up to 60 s late on purpose, RandomizedDelaySec), while pip was putting the package back: the tester's turn died at once (exit 2, "can't open file '/opt/snipetech/.venv/bin/snipetech'"), and the 03:45 round paid the operator 0.20 $ to look at "loop stage failed: operate agent --role tester". Now the ops server refuses every new job (409, nothing started) while an update's job has not ended (`ops.UPDATE_OUT`, at most 5 minutes; an update takes 4 to 5 s), and a second update while one goes out, force or not. A stage the round could not start because a deploy waits or goes out is logged "waits for the code update to go out", never "did not start": the gate no longer reads it as "loop stages not starting", so no operator turn is paid for it, and the stage starts again a round later (the agents' turns are due every round). NOT DONE: the round's own process can still start inside those seconds (the timer cannot be held from the ops server); it read no half-written module so far.

STATE (20 Nov 03:05 UTC). QUALIFY LOOKS AT WALLETS WHILE THEY STILL TRADE (the pipeline audit's first finding, scratchpad audit20/pipeline.md; tests/test_qualify_idle.py). Before: 107,634 of 107,813 candidate verdicts came back after the same 72 h; 11 of the 29 wallets on the forward check were picked after a day or more without a trade (the 2UgKTq group traded about 1,800 times on 17 Nov after a "1 coin" look and was measured 50 h after it stopped), 14 of them no longer traded, and a pending forward check never ended without 20 copies, so each held its actor's place for good. Now (qualify/gates.py): (a) a wallet short of copies, before the lab (too few coins) or after it (the sample gate), is looked at again when its own pace on the tape would have brought what it lacks, between the lab interval (2 h) and 72 h (`sample_review_hours`; hours of tape, holes left out); (b) a wallet every in-sample gate passes is picked only with a copy in the last 24 h of tape, else a candidate with the reason "passes every gate but is not picked ...", looked at again in 6 h (`not_picked_idle`); (c) a pending forward check that saw no copy for 48 h of tape ends ("forward check ended: ..."), as a candidate with no forward check, so `pick_times` ends the pick, `standing_wallets` and `release` free its actor's place, and a new pick starts once it passes while copying; a loss since its first pick still retires it first. Both are gate "idle" (counted as `idle` in the run's answer; one of NEW_RULE_GATES, so a wallet the book copied before the 7 Nov rules is never stopped by it). Two old tests changed with the rule, each said in the test: the "too few" verdict now comes back at its pace (4.5 h, not 72), and a waiting wallet in the armed-cadence test was given copies from the last 90 minutes (its data had it idle 25 h). What it will not do: make copying pay (the agents' I337); it lets the forward check answer while a wallet still trades.

STATE (19 Nov 15:55 UTC). OPENROUTER: Amazon is parked. The owner submitted the Anthropic use-case form in the Stockholm console (it went through there), and the playground then answered for Claude Fable 5.2 "AccessDeniedException: anthropic.claude-fable-5-2 is not available for this account ... contact AWS Sales": an account-level block on a new account that only AWS's support lifts (AWS's troubleshooting page; re:Post threads report days without an answer). Read the same hour, for whoever takes Amazon up again: AWS's model cards serve Opus 5.5 and Sonnet 5.5 on bedrock-mantle only in us-east-1 (Opus) and GovCloud (Sonnet), and all three models on bedrock-runtime from eu-north-1 under global.anthropic.<model> ids, which the key's default policy (AmazonBedrockLimitedAccess) covers: the mantle half of claude_client is wrong for this account and every model should go through bedrock-runtime; Fable 5.2 also needs the account's data retention mode set to aws_review (PUT https://bedrock.<region>.amazonaws.com/data-retention with the key), and the server's install has no botocore (the SDK's bedrock extra). None of that is built: the account cannot use the models. BUILT INSTEAD: with an OPENROUTER_API_KEY in /etc/snipetech/env every agent goes through OpenRouter's Anthropic-shaped endpoint (claude_client.route: OpenRouter's key first, then Amazon's, then the direct API; SNIPETECH_CLAUDE_VIA names another way). Model ids anthropic/claude-fable-5.2, -opus-5.5, -sonnet-5.5 (its public list, 19 Nov; first-party prices, no markup on use, 5.5 % on buying credit); Anthropic's own servers only (provider.only); no server-side fallback; only OpenRouter's key is sent there. The ledger records OpenRouter's own charge (usage.cost) times 1.055; the credit alarm names openrouter.ai/settings/credits; `snipetech doctor --claude` sends one small request per model, shaped like a turn's, and says what the way in answered (with a dummy key OpenRouter answered 401 "User not found": the address and the key format are understood). NOT YET SEEN: a real answer through OpenRouter (it needs the owner's key). Run `remote --wait run doctor --claude` the minute the key is in, before a round runs on it; then read the first turns (cache reads, the charge against the ledger, any 400 that names a field). THE OWNER'S STEPS (told 19 Nov): sign up at openrouter.ai, add credit (Auto Top-Up on), create a key, run the install one-liner (it asks only for that key), /budget. Three readers were reviewing this change when this was written; what they find is the next commit.

STATE (19 Nov 15:33 UTC). AMAZON BEDROCK, THE REGION: the owner's AWS account is of AWS's new sign-up kind ("projects", managed at settings.aws.com; Paid Plan since 19 Nov; advanced features NOT activated, which is irreversible and was not advised). Its home Region is Stockholm (eu-north-1), set by the contact country and not changeable. AWS manages its policies (docs.aws.amazon.com/accounts/latest/reference/scps-and-rcps-for-projects.html, read 19 Nov): in us-east-1 and us-west-2 everything is refused except a short list (Bedrock inference: InvokeModel, CountTokens, the bedrock-mantle calls and bedrock-mantle:CallWithBearerToken; NOT bedrock:PutUseCaseForModelAccess and NOT bedrock:CallWithBearerToken); every other Region outside the home one takes only those inference calls; in the home Region the plan policy allows bedrock:*, bedrock-mantle:* and aws-marketplace:*. So the Anthropic use-case form failed twice from the US East console with an explicit deny (the instruction of 17 Nov, US East, was wrong), and an Amazon key reaches bedrock-runtime only in Stockholm. FIXED: the default Region is eu-north-1 (AWS_REGION overrides). A spend limit on that account is optional (AWS Settings, Billing, Set limit); near it AWS refuses Bedrock calls (DenyBedrockUsage). THE OWNER'S STEPS (told 19 Nov): the form again in the Stockholm console, a playground test, a long-term API key, the install one-liner on the server, /budget in Telegram. NOT YET KNOWN, to read off the first turns: whether Fable 5.2's global profile is served from eu-north-1 (if not: SigV4 keys for us-east-1, since the API key is refused on bedrock-runtime there); whether the key's policy covers bedrock-mantle (else the owner attaches Amazon's mantle inference policy); whether the new account's Claude quotas are zero (many reports on re:Post, eu-north-1 among them; an AWS support case then). THE NON-COIN RULE AFTER ITS SAFETY REVIEW (commit 03e4dbd, deployed 07:30 with --when-quiet, full suite green): an independent read of 8a642d1 found no path that stops a holder, and four edge cases, each fixed with a failing test first: a refused wallet whose sniper still runs keeps its place in the ranking (a refusal must never add a sniper: before, the next wallet moved up and a stopped sniper of an actor mate could be started beside it); a failed read of the tape adds nothing in that run (before, one failed read ranked the refused wallet again and started its stopped sniper); a live pause is still recorded for a refused wallet; qualify's verdict carries no forward block (it holds no actor's place) and is due again after the 7-day window. SEEN 07:35: the book skips FvHwFe with the reason and keeps FLPjyS's sniper. FvHwFe's verdict still reads qualified until qualify reviews it; the book refuses it regardless. THE TAPE'S MISSING TIPS, AFTER TWO INDEPENDENT REVIEWS OF wf/pay-dests (8218528): the recorder path is sound (nothing can stop or mis-write the tape with the switch off; 37,440 mutated inputs gave the old result every time), five smaller defects there. The classification is NOT yet safe to apply: one wallet's payments can decide a destination's kind (a real tip account un-tipped by 90 dust payments; a fee account turned 'tip' for about 1 SOL of base fees), ten wallets of one operator sending a flat amount to their own account read as a tip account after 40 trades, an account that gets both a fee and tips reads 'tip', and a learned account can be lost to eviction. DECIDED AND SENT BACK TO THE HELPER (brief: scratchpad tool-briefs/pay-dests-round2.md): learned tips also go to their own column trades.tip_learned (undoable, and `tip` stays the whole bid); applying needs 25 distinct wallets in the sample and 'tip' at two readings in a row; one wallet's share of a sample is capped; a mixed account is never 'tip'; learned rows are protected; `tips --sample` draws transactions uniformly; honest wording. Not merged, not deployed. THE AGENTS' TWO CODE IDEAS. I159 (the hunt paper record had no trade from 05:09 to 06:35): NOT A STALL, answered and closed. Reading the rule off the tape by hand gives the record's seven trades of 04:00-05:09 exactly, and two triggers for 05:09-06:35: 06:35:24 (recorded) and 05:24:16, a coin with two trades in its life (its creator bought 9.8 SOL and sold 93 s later), which lab and paper leave out because a coin needs five trades to be replayed; 382 launches had a big first-second buy in that stretch but more than two buyers or a creator buy above 1 SOL. OPEN FROM IT: coins with under five trades are left out by lab and paper alike, and live we would buy them and pay a round trip; how often is being measured, then they are to be counted and charged (rule 2). I161 (one lab gave +12.8 and +20.1 SOL two hours apart): a lab's window always ends at the end of the hourly copy it reads and its test is the newest 40 % of moments, so two labs hours apart score different trades and no lab can be repeated; a helper builds `lab --until T` (a fixed window, proven leak-free and byte-identical), the window and the cut in every output, `lab --trades` and digests (branch wf/lab-until, brief tool-briefs/lab-until.md). THE DASHBOARD IS STALE SINCE 13 Nov 20:18: publishing to it now waits for the owner's approval click, and a call that waits blocks the session (05:11 to 06:58 today, two rounds lost); it is refreshed when the owner is present. Server on 03e4dbd, tape 6 s behind.

- THE SERVER'S SETTINGS FILE is a copy of every default of install day: a default changed in the code does not reach it. `remote --wait run doctor --settings` lists what differs. RETIRED_DEFAULTS / RETIRED_BOUNDS in config.py make an old default follow the new one; tests/test_config_drift.py fails for any default changed later until it is retired or declared the owner's. Whenever a default changes: retire the old value in the same commit.
- RECORDER: 0.06-0.1 of one core. Coverage `tape:work` holds its counters once a minute. It profiles itself on request: `remote put <file with the seconds> profile-tape`, then read `jobs/tape-profile-<time>.log`.
- THE AGENTS' ROUND: 2.5 s (it was 42 s: the paper report); job records are read by file time (`jobfiles.py`), not all 1,656 of them several times a round.

- ONE ROOT STEP, the owner's: `sudo bash /opt/snipetech/deploy/setup.sh` stops Ubuntu's updater from killing our jobs after a deploy (the nightly job died on three of seven mornings, last on 11 Nov 06:04), makes its update job gentle (it froze the machine for 11 minutes on 11 Nov) and protects half the memory for our services. Mention it once a day at most; nothing is lost for good without it.
- DISCOVERY: the tape's own source and the Birdeye ranking look every six hours (coverage `discover:last`); the other five at every run. Confirmed: the 13:07 run took 8.9 s of processor (6 min 10 s to 6 min 28 s before). Correct fact: the two slowed sources bring 0 to 2 new wallets a run; every third run the first-buyers source (`coinfirst`, refreshes every 3 h) brings 400 to 800. Never say "a run finds 0 to 2 new wallets".
- EVENTS: `dev_transfer` rows are no longer written (they were 6.4 of the 6.6 million events); the creator-to-wallet links (`edges`, kind paid_by_dev) are written in batches, a payment once a scan; each `events scan` deletes 400,000 of the old rows. The page and the store read events a kind at a time off the (kind, ts) index. The scan counts creators the RPC refused (`failed`) and holds its place when half or more were refused. The wallet gate no longer passes a wallet when its read of the links fails.

OPS NOTES: `call(url, key, "read", path=..., tail=N)` returns the file's last N BYTES from a whole line (200,000 unless told; a single line longer than N comes back as nothing) as {path, bytes, content, cut}: `cut` true means the file is longer and this is its end only (from the command line: `remote read <path> --tail N`); `logs` returns {unit, log}; `sql` allows WITH; a query with both MIN and MAX over one kind of event scans the kind (8.7 s): ask for them separately. On trades force the join order: `WITH x AS MATERIALIZED (...) SELECT ... FROM x CROSS JOIN trades t INDEXED BY trades_mint ON t.mint = x.mint AND t.slot ...`; a wallet's rows: `FROM trades INDEXED BY trades_user WHERE user = ...`.
DASHBOARD (https://claude.ai/artifact/HbtWazUA7gNP9phU8fddNZ), every round: `cd /home/user/snipetech && DASH_OUT=/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/e4403109-fa5e-5b10-9649-38580af733e4/scratchpad/dash .venv/bin/python tools/dashboard_snapshot.py`; upload .../scratchpad/dash/snapshot.json with the Artifact tool (publish, url above, asset true); ArtifactData (ToolSearch select:ArtifactData) one batch `update` of the eight datasets, each with the whole source object (format json, kind file, name snapshot.json, url "/_blob/<new id>"), updated {at now, by "Claude (hourly autopilot)"}, edit {at now, via "claude"}, if_version = its current version. proxima and proxima_split only after a fresh `launch-tools` scan. Do NOT call Artifact delete on old blobs.
TAPE: every round `remote sql "SELECT strftime('%s','now') - MAX(ts) FROM trades"` (corrected by the audit of 22 Nov: with `WHERE ts > strftime('%s','now') - 600` a recorder silent for 10 minutes or more gave an empty answer, not the lag; without it the query is one index lookup, 1 s on 23 Nov); over 300 s: `remote logs snipetech-tape` ("Unable to verify project" is Helius refusing the stream: it retries by itself; the tape replays up to 40 hours afterwards).
Each round: 1. Server reachable. 2. Tape. 3. Dashboard. 4. Open work. 5. Spend today in one line. A round with nothing new for the owner sends one line at most. When the state changed, edit THIS FILE (the STATE and OPEN WORK parts above), commit and push it in the same round; the scheduled task's own text is short and only points here.

RULES: never change wallets, move SOL, change the guard or the automatic test, or start, stop or arm a sniper from this round without the owner's yes in chat for that step; never stop a sniper that holds a coin; tests green by exit code before any push; at most two labs at a time; secrets only in /etc/snipetech/env over the owner's SSH; the server's agents may spend the remaining PumpFunData history credits since the owner's yes of 12 Nov (before: never). SUB-AGENTS AND WORKFLOWS ARE ALLOWED AGAIN since the owner's yes of 12 Nov 16:35 UTC (the scheduled round's text still says otherwise: this line is the newer word).
